The gate's real PostgreSQL expired-commit-lease probe confirmed correct fencing:
zero writes, operation NEEDS_RECONCILIATION with owner/lease cleared, linked
RUNNING tool call, no artifacts/completed events, both callers returned the
stable ToolInvocationError, and no deadlock. Uncertainty is retained explicitly;

Probe source/output are preserved beside the gate report.

### Integrated G01/G02 verification

The bounded worker repair now keeps the UoW inside a retained processing task.
After lease loss, tick returns within the cancellation-start bound even when a
handler suppresses cancellation. The processing task owns rollback/session close
until that handler finishes; `Worker.drain()` awaits retained cleanup and late
exceptions are observed. PostgreSQL assertions await actual drain before checking
the late-usage receipt and unchanged recovery state.

### G01 closed; G02 lifetime repair

The same Sol reviewer returned a bounded handoff: **A17-G01 CLOSED** with exact
stale-restart authorization preserved. It confirmed G02's delivery-generation
fences, recovery disposition and late-usage semantics, but left **G02 OPEN**:
retained cancellation-suppressing tasks could accumulate as the worker continued
claiming commands. Its inspection had not included the later shutdown wiring.

The primary reproduced a new claim during retained cleanup, then added a guard
before `claim_next`: a retained task occupies the worker slot until its UoW
closes. A regression proves no claim occurs during drain and polling resumes
afterward. The 21 entrypoint/worker/PostgreSQL lease cases pass (39.69s), with
Ruff, format and scoped mypy passing. Evidence is in
`task17-review/draining-capacity-red.log` and `draining-capacity-green.log`.
The same gate has one bounded G02 follow-up for this guard and shutdown ordering
(repair round 2); G01 and the already-confirmed core fences are not reopened.

acceptance, deployment or bootstrap runtime modification has been authorized.

nonblocking suggestions do not independently prevent publication. Passing review
freezes a readable PR body, canonical runner-result evidence (including image,
mode and network settings), and exact PR approval evidence before opening the
human gate. No remote operation is queued or authorized by reaching this gate.

This verified checkpoint batch starts at pushed `2fb1cc3`. Production composition

The HTTP-to-worker test uses real PostgreSQL, managed Git, controlled file writes
and commits, a registered Python check script and durable trusted-host audits;

This test exposed four production integration defects that isolated service tests

test additionally proved that substituting a generated branch could bypass that
event; the repaired check retains the event's authority even after substitution.
Operational file writes and checks now retain a capability pinned to the current
descendant commit. Environment staging still requires the approved base commit,
and changing HEAD during an operational capability is rejected. That runner and
environment suite passed **68 tests, 6 host skips (78.03s)**. Trusted-host result
verification now expects truthful network availability, while Docker still uses
the exact policy flag. Finally, evidence-read audit metadata nests the producing
manifest separately from the consuming tool execution, preserving both step IDs.

Dependency authorization now compares actual controlled-Git base/HEAD manifest
contents with exact `dependency:v1:` declarations in the existing approved string
field. Declarations bind path, group, package and before/after values. Supported
static Python and Node manifests may be nested; unknown formats, lockfiles and
ambiguous dependency controls require intervention. Planner prompt version 2 and
Developer version 4 describe the contract without changing approval serialization.
The parser uses `packaging.Requirement`, already locked at 26.3, now declared as a
direct dependency. Updating the existing lock graph's root dependency metadata
allowed **`uv lock --check --offline` to pass** without changing resolved versions.

The primary reassessed two incomplete dependency handoffs: path-only approval
allowed unexpected packages, and handwritten requirement parsing could hide
duplicate normalized names. Repairs use exact package deltas and a real parser.
Five further RED tests exposed case aliases, requirements variants, substituted
snapshot paths and unsupported build/bundling controls; the resulting focused
suite passed **16 tests**. Six controlled-Git snapshot tests passed, covering
regular files, HEAD drift, truncation, replacement decoding and non-regular modes.
Strict mypy passes all **167 source files**; scoped Ruff and formatting pass.

### Task18 operator controls — active 2026-09-08

The production delivery checkpoint above is preserved remotely as `2cc4679`
on `origin/forge/v0-1`. The next real-Git workflow test now submits operator
candidate feedback after reaching the PR gate. Its focused RED run failed at

Pause/cancel investigation confirmed that the existing single per-run command
lease prevents queued controls from running while a provider invocation is
active. The chosen implementation retains the API's enqueue-only boundary and
adds a separately leased worker control lane for pause/cancel. Resume stays in
the normal lane and requires reconciliation before continuation. Work is split
between command scheduling, control handlers and candidate revision; production
composition and workflow integration remain primary-owned. These are coupled
state/authority changes, so the existing integrated Task18 review and scoped
correctness gate remain justified. No additional helper reviews are being run.

Retained-outcome recovery checkpoint (base `70759d3`, Task18 remains active):
The actual pending-pause Planner path reproduced a recovery failure after its
known result was stored: no `delivery.suspended` receipt existed and the original
execution remained RUNNING (`.llm-output/task18-retained-resume-red.log`, one
expected failure, 2.14s). Partial validation similarly retained a successful
first-check receipt but could not resume (`task18-retained-validation-red.log`,
one expected unsupported-source failure, 2.62s).

The primary integrated and completed the bounded worker's partial implementation.
Resume now has the production artifact store and can reconcile an expired source
whose known Planner/Developer/Reviewer outcome is retained. Recovery verifies
canonical immutable receipt bytes, digest/schema/lineage, execution identity,
known result or failure, and measured aggregate/attempt usage bound to admission.
It conditionally cancels the observed expired delivery, finalizes only a RUNNING
original execution as CANCELLED, writes the stop receipt, and proves whole-run
quiescence before the existing atomic continuation/restore. Missing evidence,
renewed source leases, substituted command provenance, and unresolved operations
leave the paused run and original execution unchanged. This never publishes the
old outcome as success or reuses its execution ID. Known failures remain distinct
from missing/unknown outcomes.

Controller recovery verifies the exact admitted validation step/event and pinned
policy before cancellation; completed operation receipts remain retained. The
fresh validation attempt reruns every required check. Pending operation effects
and changed admission events roll the entire recovery transaction back.

### A18-C04 failed-before-admission continuation checkpoint — 2026-09-08

A18-C04 is REPAIRED, pending the integrated findings recheck. A transaction-bound
failed-command query and `failed_resume.py` now derive a continuation only from
one FAILED command matching the suspended stage and predecessor version. They
verify the current resume lease and completed causal pause, require unchanged
semantic admission count and whole-run quiescence, and preserve the original
FAILED command. Older failed commands from earlier versions do not block a later
recovery. A distinct exact `delivery.failed_before_admission` receipt preserves
source identity, actor, payload, schema, delivery/semantic attempts and pause/state.

The continuation allocator, historical origin-chain reader and resume-handler
replay verify this receipt before accepting FAILED source status. The fresh
command keeps the same semantic attempt because the source was never admitted;
ordinary fresh stage admission still verifies its approved inputs. The handler
calls this route only when normal reconciliation returns no source. Preparing
worktree recovery retains its separate existing resource-proof route.

Focused TDD reproduced the missing route (`resume has no unique reconciled local
stage`, 1 expected failure2.28s), `.llm-output/task18-c04-red.log`. Final focused
suite: **10 passed (15.16s)**, `task18-c04-checks.log`. Cases cover one/two consecutive
failed Developer deliveries, handler replay, planning/validation/review dispatch,
immutable FAILED history, unchanged semantic attempt, altered receipt rejection,
unresolved effects, running/settled admissions and ambiguous failed sources.
Adversarial fixture construction initially needed terminal timestamps and priced

### A18-C01/C02 published-result recovery checkpoint — 2026-09-08

C01/C02 are REPAIRED, pending integrated findings recheck and the correctness gate.
`published_resume.py` verifies already-SUCCEEDED validation/review publication
before resume reconciliation settles an expired source command. It preserves
terminal step/execution timestamps, output identities and reviewer usage, records
an explicitly published suspended receipt, and lets ordinary continuation admit
a fresh semantic attempt. Every fresh validation reruns required checks; every
fresh Reviewer receives a distinct execution with no Developer parent.

The proof binds the current approved plan and source actor, original/resumed
command payload and key, semantic attempt, exact step/execution namespaces,
producer outcome, canonical manifest bytes/digest/schema, typed evidence descriptor,
policy/check list, prior review/validation inputs and artifact lineage/parents.
It rejects any decision event naming the source. Historical command parsers use
a transient non-dispatch envelope projection; it never claims a lease. Completed
Reviewer proof uses its immutable publication and parent validation, leaving the
runtime evidence reader's RUNNING-only restriction unchanged. Unreadable/corrupt
proof becomes CommandRecoveryRequired. Existing published receipts are verified
again with artifact storage before continuation; a boolean marker alone cannot
authorize terminal-success recovery. Ordinary cancelled receipts retain their
existing format and status restrictions.

Primary replaced the incomplete Terra partial after preserving it at
`.llm-output/task18-published-partial.py`. New tests initially found six failures
against that partial (decision/actor substitution accepted; corrupt-manifest errors
not normalized) and two happy-path passes, 17.36s; `task18-published-red.log`.
The new 14-case publication suite plus retained-outcome coverage passed **27 tests
(47.43s)**, `task18-published-affected.log`. Final publication/retained/settlement/
run-resume/planning-resume selection passed **43 tests (63.97s)**,
`task18-published-final.log`. It includes publication-to-pause before decision,
fresh execution plus decision, unchanged succeeded history/usage, command actor
and decision tampering, corrupt bytes, saved-receipt replay and altered output IDs.
All pytest excludes live_provider/live_github; no paid acceptance ran.

ancestry/adoption, not rewriting the original plan approval.

Added BaseUpdateOperation for the remote phase: exact persisted request binds PR
identity, previous/new base, expected head, policy, observation and remote attempt.
Before update-branch it verifies current PR identity/head, target base, branch head
and safe protection. Acknowledgement is not sufficient: completion requires a new
head with matching PR/base/branch identity. Reconciliation observes only and never
reissues update-branch. This receipt does not itself authorize local adoption or
claim the new head includes the intended base; that requires Git ancestry proof.

Added singleton recovery_barrier model/migration0006 and PostgresRecoveryBarrier acquire/renew/finish/abandon with PostgreSQL time and owner/generation fencing. Claim admission takes a shared singleton row lock before command/run locks in the same transaction for both lanes. required=true blocks claims even after owner expiry; takeover is mandatory. Finish alone clears required; abandon leaves it closed and cannot affect a successor owner. Missing singleton fails closed.

Primary traced the remaining TD recovery-loop concern through production startup:
worker/main.py reconciles with allow_unresolved then invokes
StartupInterventionRecovery.quarantine. That records run.recovery_intervention
with operation IDs/digests even for terminal runs, preserving business state.
PostgresCommandRepository.claim_next skips ordinary commands while the shared
startup_intervention_hold predicate is true. An unresolved teardown therefore
receives a durable hold on restart rather than repeated ordinary dispatch.
The hold stops applying once authoritative work is no longer unresolved.

five SDK deprecation warnings. Includes production startup isolation, terminal
CANCELLED/FAILED and PAUSED preservation, barrier ownership and live-owner
waiting. This is evidence of existing quarantine behavior, not a new branch-

GitHub's current rules API documents merge_queue.parameters.merge_method as
MERGE/SQUASH/REBASE. MergeProtection now retains normalized merge_queue_method;
effective queue rules with invalid or conflicting methods are unverified. Both
user and installation credential paths preserve it. Existing asdict protection
digests therefore bind the queue method. Adding the nullable field changes newly
computed protection digests, so an older pending approval must be revalidated;
no historical stored evidence is rewritten. Historical receipt recovery remains
separate from new-effect preflight.
